- Select one donated equipment item and one donated professional service, and document how their recognition and disclosure assessments differ under the applicable framework.
Key point:
- Non-cash contributions need delivery, ownership or control, and valuation evidence as applicable.
- UN-specific recognition thresholds are not universal NGO rules.
Source links:
- UN IPSAS Finance Manual, Chapter 3 — Contributions in kind
- UN IPSAS Finance Manual, Chapter 8 — Donated assets
21. Q: What checks are required before issuing a donor acknowledgement or receipt?
A: Show answer
The issuer should verify the approved receipt record, recipient identity, amount, currency, date, purpose, donor or remitter attribution and permitted acknowledgement type. The wording should accurately describe the facts established by available evidence and must not imply a particular accounting classification, spending eligibility or tax benefit without proper authorization. The organization should assign controlled reference numbers, restrict issuance rights, retain the final document, record delivery and link corrections or cancellations to the original acknowledgement. An acknowledgement should not be issued simply because a donor sent a notification or because an amount appears in a provisional report. Unresolved identity, settlement or legal questions should trigger a hold until the required approval and evidence are available.
Study instruction:
- Inspect a sample acknowledgement and trace its amount, recipient, reference number, approval, issue date and delivery evidence to the verified receipt record.
Key point:
- An acknowledgement confirms only facts and representations that have been properly established and approved.
- Issued documents require traceable numbering and correction history.
Source links:
- UN IPSAS Finance Manual, Chapter 3 — Voluntary contributions
22. Q: What should the organization do when a donor requests a statement that the donation is tax-deductible or tax-exempt?
A: Show answer
The request should be referred to the authorized tax or legal specialist. The organization must verify the actual entity's legal status, the applicable tax law, the donor and transaction facts, and any requirements governing the particular claim. Charitable purpose, nonprofit status or receipt of a donation alone does not establish that the donor qualifies for a deduction or that the organization may issue a particular tax document. Until the required authority and evidence have been confirmed, the organization may issue only a factual acknowledgement that uses approved wording and does not imply an unverified tax benefit. Any approved special wording should have an identified owner, legal basis, applicable scope and review date.
Study instruction:
- Review an acknowledgement request containing a tax claim and prepare the evidence and approvals required before the wording can be authorized.
Key point:
- Tax treatment and deductible-donation status must not be inferred from organizational labels.
- Unsupported tax claims must be blocked until specialist clearance is documented.
Source links:
- UN IPSAS Finance Manual, Chapter 3 — Revenue from non-exchange transactions
23. Q: How should unidentified or unmatched incoming funds be handled?
A: Show answer
An incoming credit with an unknown donor, unclear purpose, conflicting currency or amount, or missing agreement reference should be registered as an exception and assigned to a responsible owner. Preserve the verified cash movement, but do not invent a donor identity, force an agreement match, issue an unsupported donor acknowledgement or authorize an allocation simply to clear the register. The owner should request corroborating remittance details, review available source reports, contact the appropriate intermediary through authenticated channels and record each investigative action. Any accounting treatment required while the funds remain unidentified must be determined by the authorized accountant under the applicable framework and policy. The case should remain open or on hold until the evidence and approved resolution are recorded.
Study instruction:
- Build an investigation record for an unidentified bank credit showing the facts established, unanswered questions, inquiry history, owner, next action and closure evidence.
Key point:
- Unidentified funds require controlled investigation, not invented attribution.
- A verified bank credit may remain unresolved for other process decisions.
Source links:
- UN IPSAS Finance Manual, Chapter 3 — Non-exchange revenue and contributions
24. Q: How should ageing be used to monitor unidentified and unmatched receipts?
A: Show answer
The register should calculate ageing from a consistently defined date, such as the date the credit first appeared in the source record, while retaining settlement and investigation dates separately. Cases should be grouped by approved age bands and assessed for amount, risk, donor sensitivity, compliance concerns and the likelihood of resolution. Each overdue case needs an assigned owner, documented inquiries, a next action and an escalation date. Ageing is a monitoring tool, not an accounting decision. An old unidentified balance should not automatically become revenue, be written off, be allocated to unrestricted funds or be treated as closed. Resolution requires appropriate evidence and approval for the specific action being taken.
Study instruction:
- Test the ageing schedule for unresolved receipts and check whether each overdue item has an owner, next action, escalation record and supported disposition.
Key point:
- Ageing prioritizes investigation and management attention.
- The passage of time does not authorize recognition, write-off or closure.
Source links:
- UN IPSAS Finance Manual, Chapter 16 — Receivables and recoverability
25. Q: What controls are needed when donations arrive as physical cash rather than through a bank or payment platform?
A: Show answer
Physical cash requires channel-specific controls for custody, counting, documentation, deposit and reconciliation. Two authorized individuals should verify the amount where required by the organization's approved policy, with the collection date, source, purpose, denomination details and custody transfer recorded. Cash should be secured, deposited through an approved channel within the applicable timeframe and compared with the resulting bank credit. Any shortage, overage, delayed deposit or break in custody must be reported and investigated. Cash should not be mixed with personal funds or used for expenditure before the authorized procedures are followed. The control design should account for fundraising events, field collections, sealed collection boxes and other collection methods that create different evidence and security risks.
Study instruction:
- Walk through a physical cash collection from initial count to bank deposit and identify who holds, verifies, deposits and reconciles the funds.
Key point:
- Cash collections require documented custody and independent count controls.
- Deposit and reconciliation evidence must connect to the original collection record.
Source links:
- UN IPSAS Finance Manual, Chapter 18 — General Ledger and cash-related records
26. Q: How can the organization demonstrate that all incoming receipts have been captured in the register?
A: Show answer
Completeness requires obtaining the full population of relevant source records for the reporting period, including applicable bank accounts, payment providers, cash collection records and other approved receiving channels. The reviewer should document the accounts and channels covered, date range, extraction method, report filters, transaction count and control totals. Each source line should be matched to one receipt event or have an approved explanation for why it is not a receipt within scope. The reverse direction also matters: each receipt register item should be traceable to a valid source event or another approved basis. Missing accounts, excluded report pages, failed imports and unprocessed returns must be resolved before the period can be certified complete.
Study instruction:
- Reconcile an entire period's source population to the receipt register in both directions and document every missing or out-of-scope item.
Key point:
- Source population completeness must be demonstrated, not assumed.
- Total agreement alone cannot establish that all source records were included.
Source links:
- UN IPSAS Finance Manual, Chapter 18 — General Ledger records and reconciliation
27. Q: How should returns, reversals, chargebacks and other settlement adjustments be processed?
A: Show answer
Each adjustment should be recorded as a new linked event that preserves the original receipt, amount, date, reference and previous status. Finance should verify the actual bank or provider transaction, the reason for the adjustment and any relevant agreement or contractual basis. It should then assess whether allocation, acknowledgement, accounting posting, donor communication and previous reconciliation are affected. A reversal or refund must not be recorded solely because someone reports that funds were returned; evidence of the actual transaction or other authorized resolution is required. Corrective accounting entries must be prepared and approved through the applicable accounting process. The event history should connect the original transaction, the adjustment, the resulting balance and the final supporting evidence.
Study instruction:
- Trace a returned donation from the original bank credit through the reversal evidence, linked correction record, accounting adjustment and final reconciliation.
Key point:
- Adjustments must preserve the history of the original receipt.
- Reported returns and confirmed settlement reversals are different states.
Source links:
- UN IPSAS Finance Manual, Chapter 18 — Journal entries and reversals
28. Q: What controls should apply before a donation is refunded to a donor?
A: Show answer
A refund request should be supported by a legitimate contractual or legal basis, verified identity of the entitled payee, confirmed refund amount, appropriate delegated approval and an approved payment channel. Treasury should verify changed account details independently through a previously authenticated contact method rather than relying solely on an email accompanying the request. The organization should assess whether the proposed payment duplicates an earlier return or would conflict with a prior settlement adjustment. After authorization, the actual payment evidence must be linked to the original receipt and refund case, followed by the necessary accounting and reconciliation updates. Requests involving disputed ownership, suspicious instructions or unclear contractual rights should remain on hold until resolved by the authorized reviewer.
Study instruction:
- Test a refund scenario involving changed bank details and specify the independent verification, legal basis, approval and settlement evidence required.
Key point:
- A refund requires verified entitlement, payee details and delegated authorization.
- Suspicious account changes must be held rather than processed urgently without verification.
Source links:
- UN IPSAS Finance Manual, Chapter 18 — Financial transactions and journal controls
29. Q: How should changes to donor details, bank details, agreement terms or receipt parameters be controlled?
A: Show answer
A change should be requested by an authorized party, supported by evidence, independently verified where appropriate and approved under the relevant delegation. The change record should identify the previous and new values, effective date, reason, requester, reviewer, approver, source document and systems affected. Changes to bank details or donor identities require particular care because they may redirect refunds, alter attribution or affect compliance screening. Existing transactions should retain their original context and link to the applicable version of the master data. A corrected master record should not silently rewrite the history of a previously issued acknowledgement or posted transaction. Periodic review should identify unauthorized changes, stale records and parameters whose approval has expired.
Study instruction:
- Inspect a sample master-data change and verify the request, evidence, independent approval, effective date and downstream impact assessment.
Key point:
- Master-data changes require controlled approval and traceable history.
- Historical transaction evidence must remain intact after a change.
Source links:
- UN IPSAS Finance Manual, Chapter 18 — General Ledger master data and controls
30. Q: How should a receipt be referred for accounting without allowing the receipt-processing team to decide recognition automatically?
A: Show answer
The receipt-processing team should assemble the verified facts, source evidence, donor agreement, restrictions, allocation decision and unresolved questions into a controlled accounting referral. The authorized accountant then determines the appropriate treatment under the applicable framework and policy. The referral should distinguish established facts from assumptions and should identify any conditions, uncertainty or legal interpretation that needs additional specialist input. Accounting must return an approved disposition with a clear basis and reference before the receipt is posted. The workflow should prevent a receipt processor from selecting an accounting category merely because it is available in a spreadsheet or system dropdown. This separation preserves professional judgment and reduces the risk that operational classifications inadvertently determine financial statement presentation.
Study instruction:
- Prepare an accounting referral for an unfamiliar donation and document which facts are verified, which decisions remain open and who has authority to resolve them.
Key point:
- Receipt verification provides facts; accounting authorization determines treatment.
- An automated category or accepted referral is not evidence of an approved accounting conclusion.
Source links:
- UN IPSAS Finance Manual, Chapter 3 — Revenue from non-exchange transactions
31. Q: What evidence is required before a receipt can be marked as posted to the general ledger?
A: Show answer
A prepared journal, submitted interface request or successful file upload does not by itself prove that a general ledger posting occurred. The process should obtain authoritative evidence from the actual accounting system showing the posted document or transaction reference, relevant line details, posting date, amount, account and successful final status. Where the accounting system supports workflow approval, the approval result should also be retained. Failed, pending or partially completed attempts must be recorded separately so they cannot be mistaken for final postings. The receipt register should link the actual accounting reference to the original receipt and approved accounting referral. If the evidence is unavailable, the status must remain pending or otherwise reflect the verified system state.
Study instruction:
- Compare a prepared journal, an interface acceptance response and an actual posted ledger document, then identify which evidence supports each status.
Key point:
- A posting attempt is not an actual posting.
- Final status requires verifiable evidence from the authoritative accounting system.
Source links:
- UN IPSAS Finance Manual, Chapter 18 — General Ledger documents and workflows
32. Q: What should happen when an accounting interface fails after a transaction may already have been posted?
A: Show answer
The system owner should first determine the actual state of the original attempt using the source reference, batch identifier, accounting document number or other authoritative query. A missing response does not necessarily mean that no posting occurred. Re-submitting the transaction blindly could create a duplicate entry. Record the initial attempt, error response, state investigation, any partial posting, corrective authorization and final result. If correction or retry is needed, it should follow the approved recovery procedure with duplicate protection and independent review. The receipt must remain pending or held until the actual ledger state is established and linked to the register. The workflow should allow failed and partial states to remain visible instead of falsely marking them complete.
Study instruction:
- Simulate an interface timeout after submission and determine the evidence needed before retrying or correcting the transaction.
Key point:
- Confirm the actual system state before retrying a failed or uncertain interface.
- Partial execution requires explicit investigation and duplicate prevention.
Source links:
- UN IPSAS Finance Manual, Chapter 18 — General Ledger workflows
33. Q: How should the receipt register be reconciled with the complete bank or provider population and the actual general ledger?
A: Show answer
Reconciliation should compare source transactions with receipt records and actual general ledger entries in both directions. The procedure must identify source lines without register references, register records without valid source evidence, and register items without appropriate accounting disposition or posting evidence. Differences should be analyzed by account, transaction, amount, currency, date, status and reference. Fees, foreign exchange effects, returns, reversals, duplicates, timing differences and items not yet eligible for posting must be separately explained. A zero balance difference is not sufficient if the source report omits an account or the register contains unsupported items. An independent reviewer should sign off only after the scope, completeness, outstanding exceptions and final dispositions are adequately evidenced.
Study instruction:
- Prepare a three-way reconciliation and test both directions, including source completeness, unmatched receipt records and actual general ledger references.
Key point:
- Reconciliation must establish population completeness as well as monetary agreement.
- Unresolved differences require an owner, due date and supported disposition.
Source links:
- UN IPSAS Finance Manual, Chapter 18 — General Ledger and reconciliation controls
34. Q: How should receipt cut-off and period-end completeness be established?
A: Show answer
The organization should define the reporting period and obtain complete source records covering the required cut-off dates. It should identify transactions initiated before period-end but settled later, pending credits, reversals, unprocessed cash collections, unidentified receipts and postings made in a subsequent period. The event date, settlement date, recognition date and posting date may differ, and the relevant accounting treatment depends on the applicable framework. The procedure should not force these dates to coincide simply to close a period. Finance must document material timing differences, assign follow-up responsibility and refer accounting questions for resolution. The period should not be certified complete until all expected source populations have been examined and outstanding exceptions have been appropriately resolved or formally escalated.
Study instruction:
- Test transactions around month-end and identify which dates establish settlement, accounting recognition and ledger posting under the applicable policy.
Key point:
- Cut-off must follow the applicable accounting requirements and supported transaction facts.
- A period-end certification requires evidence of completeness and disposition of outstanding items.
Source links:
- UN IPSAS Finance Manual, Chapter 16 — Financial statement presentation and revenue information
35. Q: How should suspicious donation patterns or possible compliance concerns be handled?
A: Show answer
The procedure should identify risk indicators such as unexplained third-party remittances, inconsistent donor identities, unusual payment routes, repeated attempts to change refund destinations, conflicting agreements, suspected duplicate transfers and unexplained source-of-funds information. These indicators justify investigation but do not automatically establish wrongdoing. The organization should assess which legal, sanctions, anti-money-laundering, counter-terrorist-financing and reporting requirements apply to its actual entity, activities and channels. The authorized compliance owner should determine whether additional verification, restricted handling or escalation is required. Sensitive investigations should be limited to appropriate personnel, documented securely and handled under approved procedures. Staff should not bypass controls because a donation is urgently needed or the remitter is considered reputable.
Study instruction:
- Examine a hypothetical receipt with conflicting identity and refund instructions and map the required investigation, escalation and authorization steps.
Key point:
- Red flags require risk-based investigation, not automatic accusations or clearance.
- Compliance obligations must be determined for the actual entity and transaction.
Source links:
- Government Regulation No. 29 of 1980 — Implementation of Fundraising
36. Q: How should donor and payment information be protected under Indonesia's personal data framework?
A: Show answer
The organization should identify which personal data it collects, why it is needed, who may access it, how it is protected and how long it must be retained. The workflow should restrict donor identity, financial information, payment evidence and investigation details to authorized personnel with a legitimate operational need. Data shared with consultants, payment platforms or other service providers should be governed by appropriate access and processing controls. The organization should also maintain procedures for correcting inaccurate records, responding to incidents, reviewing access and disposing of data when legally permitted. Indonesia's Law No. 27 of 2022 on Personal Data Protection is an important reference, but specific duties depend on the facts and applicable requirements. Records must not include passwords, authentication tokens or other secrets that are unnecessary for the transaction file.
Study instruction:
- Inventory the personal data held in receipt forms and registers, classify access rights and identify unnecessary or excessively exposed fields.
Key point:
- Donor records require controlled, purpose-appropriate processing and access.
- Financial and identity information must not be exposed unnecessarily.
Source links:
- Law No. 27 of 2022 — Personal Data Protection
37. Q: How should evidence retention, archival access and disposal be controlled?
A: Show answer
The organization should maintain an approved retention schedule that considers applicable law, accounting and audit obligations, donor agreements, contractual requirements, organizational policies and any legal hold. The schedule should identify record categories, required retention periods, permitted access, responsible custodians and authorized disposal methods. Receipt evidence should be organized so that the original source, matching work, approval, acknowledgement, accounting referral, posting and reconciliation can be retrieved together. Disposal must not occur simply because a case is closed or a file is old; the authorized records owner must verify that retention requirements and outstanding holds have been addressed. Backups should also be tested to confirm that historical records can be recovered and read when required.
Study instruction:
- Select a closed receipt case and verify the applicable retention rule, legal holds, access restrictions, archive location and recoverability of the evidence.
Key point:
- Closure does not automatically authorize disposal of supporting records.
- Retention and destruction require a verified schedule and authorized decision.
Source links:
- Law No. 27 of 2022 — Personal Data Protection
38. Q: What registers and controlled forms should support the receipt procedure?
A: Show answer
The supporting records should cover document control, entity and bank-account parameters, source applicability, settlement verification, evidence custody, receipt intake and event history, donor matching, agreement linkage, gross-to-net calculation, allocation approvals, acknowledgements, unidentified receipts, returns and refunds, accounting referrals, posting confirmation, reconciliation, exceptions, period-end certification, delegated authority, change management, retention and monitoring. Each form must have a defined purpose, owner, required fields, status domain and approval or closure criteria. Cross-references should link related records without duplicating the underlying evidence unnecessarily. The list in the sample SOP is a design proposal, not proof that the organization currently operates those controls. The adopted set should be based on actual risks and tested for usability before release.
Study instruction:
- Compare existing operating records with the proposed register inventory and identify missing controls, duplicated forms and registers that have no clear owner.
Key point:
- Every controlled form should support a defined decision or process requirement.
- An inventory of forms is not evidence that those controls operate in practice.
Source links:
- UN IPSAS Finance Manual, Chapter 18 — General Ledger records and master data
39. Q: How can Excel, Power Query and VBA improve the receipt process without replacing accounting judgment?
A: Show answer
Excel tables can provide consistent receipt registers, validation lists and controlled calculations. Power Query can combine bank, provider and remittance files, normalize fields, flag potential duplicates and prepare exception lists. VBA can support controlled exports, workflow updates and audit-log creation. These technologies can make routine checks repeatable, but they cannot independently establish a legal donor identity, the enforceability of a donor agreement, the correct accounting framework or the validity of a tax claim. The process should preserve source data, record refresh dates, compare control totals, protect critical parameters and require approval for changes to calculation logic. Automated outputs must be tested against independently verified samples before use, and exceptions must be resolved by authorized personnel rather than automatically forced into a standard category.
Study instruction:
- Test an automated import with missing identifiers, duplicate lines, different currency formats and mismatched totals, then verify that each exception is correctly reported.
Key point:
- Automation improves repeatability but does not establish the validity of the underlying transaction.
- Critical accounting, legal and authorization decisions remain subject to competent review.
Source links:
- UN IPSAS Finance Manual, Chapter 18 — Accounting control and records
40. Q: What audit-trail information should be recorded for important changes and decisions?
A: Show answer
The audit trail should identify the event or transaction, date and time, responsible user, action performed, reason, affected record, previous and new status, supporting evidence and required approval. For changes to donor identity, allocation, acknowledgements or accounting referrals, the record should preserve a link to the previous version rather than silently replace it. Posting attempts should retain their transaction references and actual outcomes, including failures and partial execution. The log itself must have restricted permissions so that users cannot freely alter the history they are expected to preserve. Review procedures should test whether critical actions can be traced to authorized individuals and whether the history is sufficiently detailed to reconstruct the decision from the original evidence.
Study instruction:
- Trace one amended receipt from its initial registration through every subsequent change, identifying who acted, why and under which approval.
Key point:
- Audit trails support reconstruction and accountability for decisions.
- Corrections should be linked to preserved history rather than overwrite the original event.
Source links:
- UN IPSAS Finance Manual, Chapter 18 — General Ledger document workflows
41. Q: How should exceptions be assigned, investigated and escalated?
A: Show answer
The exception register should record a unique case identifier, affected receipt, issue type, financial exposure where known, risk level, owner, deadline, next action, reviewer and evidence of resolution. Examples include unmatched remittances, unexpected currencies, invalid agreement references, unverified settlement, suspicious account changes, calculation differences and incomplete accounting posting. Escalation thresholds should consider urgency, value, legal or donor implications and the possibility of fraud, rather than age alone. Routine processing may continue only where doing so does not depend on the unresolved decision. Closure requires the evidence and approval applicable to that exception type. The register should also identify recurring root causes so management can correct weaknesses in process design, staffing, system configuration or source-data quality.
Study instruction:
- Review several real or simulated exceptions and determine their risk level, accountable owner, escalation route, evidence requirement and closure conditions.
Key point:
- Every material exception needs ownership, a defined next step and a defensible closure decision.
- Moving an unresolved case to the next period does not resolve it.
Source links:
- UN IPSAS Finance Manual, Chapter 18 — Financial control and journal workflows
42. Q: Which indicators can management use to evaluate the effectiveness of donation receipt controls?
A: Show answer
Useful indicators include the percentage of source lines linked to a receipt or approved non-receipt status, the value and age of unidentified credits, receipts with verified settlement evidence, gross-to-net calculation differences, overdue accounting referrals, acknowledgement corrections, reconciliation variances and recurring control exceptions. Each indicator needs a clear definition, source population, calculation method, reporting frequency, owner and escalation rule. A completeness metric should use the entire relevant source population as its denominator, not just records already processed. Metrics should distinguish pending, failed, held, posted and reconciled transactions instead of combining them into a single completion percentage. Management should review trends and investigate root causes, as well as assign corrective actions for indicators that deteriorate over time.
Study instruction:
- Calculate receipt completeness and unresolved-case ageing from the source register, then independently verify the populations and denominators used.
Key point:
- Every indicator needs a reproducible definition and reliable underlying data.
- A dashboard summarizes control performance but does not replace evidence-based review.
Source links:
- UN IPSAS Finance Manual, Chapter 16 — Financial reporting and disclosures
43. Q: What should a management report show about incoming donations and unresolved receipt balances?
A: Show answer
The report should summarize verified receipts by period, currency, channel and other approved dimensions while keeping unidentified, unmatched, unallocated, pending and held items separately visible. It should show significant changes, overdue cases, unsupported acknowledgements, accounting referrals awaiting disposition, posting backlogs and open reconciliation differences. Figures should be traceable to the receipt register and actual accounting evidence, with the reporting date and data refresh time clearly identified. Management should see the value of outstanding cases, their risk, accountable owner and next action, rather than only aggregate cash totals. Reports should be reviewed and version-controlled before distribution, and corrected versions should document what changed and who approved the revision.
Study instruction:
- Review a proposed monthly receipt report and trace its totals to the source population, register, accounting records and open-exception list.
Key point:
- Management reporting should distinguish verified facts from unresolved amounts and provisional statuses.
- Material exceptions need visible ownership and follow-up actions.
Source links:
- UN IPSAS Finance Manual, Chapter 16 — Financial statements and disclosures
44. Q: How should procedures, decision tables and controlled forms remain consistent with one another?
A: Show answer
The written procedure should state the activities, roles, evidence requirements and approvals, while decision tables define the conditions that determine permitted actions or outcomes. Forms and registers must capture the information required to execute those rules. A controlled inventory should connect each procedure step to the relevant decision rule, form, owner and evidence reference. When a rule or form changes, the organization should review all dependent documents, training materials, automated controls and reporting outputs. This prevents the written SOP from instructing users to follow a control that the operational register or system does not support. The sample document proposes a detailed inventory, but the organization must confirm that each artifact exists, is approved, and is actually used.
Study instruction:
- Trace selected procedure steps to their decision rules, form fields, workflow statuses and final evidence, identifying any mismatches.
Key point:
- Procedures, decision tables and forms should implement one coherent control design.
- A documented control is not effective merely because its form exists.
Source links:
- UN IPSAS Finance Manual, Chapter 18 — Accounting process workflows
45. Q: How should changes to legal authorities, donor agreements, accounting rules and system configuration be introduced?
A: Show answer
Assign a responsible owner to monitor authoritative sources and assess changes affecting the actual organization. Record the source, publication and effective dates, affected receipt channels, revised parameters, required procedural changes and approval authority. Do not change an accounting rule, legal claim or calculation formula based solely on an informal summary or verbal instruction. Test proposed changes against representative transactions, including normal cases and exceptions, and document the results before release. The approved version should identify the effective date and preserve the previous version for historical traceability. Users affected by the change should receive relevant training, and open transactions should be reviewed to determine whether the new rule applies to them.
Study instruction:
- Take one proposed regulatory or system change and document its impact assessment, authority, test cases, approval, release date and communication plan.
Key point:
- Changes require verified authority, testing and authorized release.
- Historical records must remain explainable under the rules that applied at the relevant time.
Source links:
- IPSASB, IPSAS 47 — Revenue
46. Q: What must be completed before a receipt-control SOP is formally adopted and released?
A: Show answer
The organization should confirm its legal identity, applicable framework, relevant legal obligations, approved receiving accounts, permitted payment channels, donor agreement requirements, tax-related authority, delegations and retention policies. It should test the complete workflow using actual authorized procedures or safely controlled test data, verify that required registers and system controls exist, and resolve significant gaps. Responsible process owners and independent reviewers should approve the document through the organization's formal governance process. The final release should include the version number, effective date, approving authority, training requirements and review cycle. Unverified organizational facts, fictitious approvals, sample bank transactions and invented general ledger references must remain clearly identified as illustrative material and must never be presented as operational evidence.
Study instruction:
- Use the proposed acceptance checklist to distinguish completed and evidenced requirements from items that remain unverified, unapproved or untested.
Key point:
- Adoption requires actual organizational authority and evidence of readiness.
- A fictional demonstration and a technically complete document do not establish operational approval.
Source links:
- UN IPSAS Finance Manual, Chapter 18 — General Ledger controls and approvals
47. Q: How should pooled donations received through a fundraising platform be handled when the underlying donors cannot yet be identified?
A: Show answer
The organization should first verify the platform's actual settlement into the approved receiving account and reconcile the net amount to the provider's gross collections, fees, reserves, refunds and other documented adjustments. If the platform reports a pooled credit without enough information to identify individual donors, the receipt should remain registered with the platform as remitter and the underlying donors marked unresolved. Finance should obtain the available transaction-level reports, donor allocation files and settlement terms, while the responsible owner evaluates what additional information is required. The organization must not infer individual donor identities from payment narratives alone. A supported accounting treatment may still be required for the funds, but donor attribution, allocation and acknowledgement must follow separate evidence and approval requirements.
Study instruction:
- Reconcile a hypothetical pooled platform settlement and identify which facts are established and which depend on additional donor-level evidence.
Key point:
- A verified platform settlement does not automatically identify the underlying donors.
- Gross-to-net reconciliation and donor-level attribution require different evidence.
Source links:
- UN IPSAS Finance Manual, Chapter 3 — Voluntary contributions
48. Q: What should happen when the receipt register and the donor agreement disagree on amount or currency?
A: Show answer
The discrepancy must be recorded and investigated before the affected donor match, allocation or acknowledgement is approved. Finance should compare the executed agreement and amendments with the remittance advice, provider settlement report, actual bank credit and correspondence from authorized donor representatives. It should determine whether the difference arises from exchange rates, fees, staged payments, a revised award, a partial transfer or an error. The preparer should document the calculations and facts, but must not change the agreement value or use an undocumented tolerance to force the records to agree. The relevant grant owner or authorized specialist must resolve contractual questions, and the accounting owner must determine any resulting accounting adjustment. All approved changes should retain links to the original documents and decision record.
Study instruction:
- Investigate a receipt that differs from its award amount and prepare a reconciliation showing the factual cause, outstanding questions and required approvals.
Key point:
- Amount and currency differences require supported explanations, not unexplained balancing adjustments.
- Contract amendments and accounting corrections require their respective authorization processes.
Source links:
- UN IPSAS Finance Manual, Chapter 3 — Funding agreements
49. Q: How should the organization treat donated equipment that arrives directly from a donor without cash entering the bank account?
A: Show answer
Record the physical receipt and preserve evidence of the donor, delivery date, item description, quantity, condition, intended purpose and any transfer documentation. Determine whether the organization controls the equipment and whether its applicable accounting framework requires recognition as an asset, expense or another category. If recognition is appropriate, the item should be referred to the authorized accounting process and linked to the fixed-asset or inventory register, with a supported valuation where required. Donor restrictions, custody arrangements and any disposal conditions should be recorded separately from the accounting classification. The absence of a cash receipt does not remove the need for operational and accounting controls. Recognition and measurement must follow the framework and policies applicable to the actual organization, not an assumed UN-specific rule.
Study instruction:
- Trace donated equipment from delivery evidence to the asset assessment, approval, inventory or fixed-asset register and final accounting reference.
Key point:
- Non-cash donations can require asset, inventory and accounting controls without bank activity.
- Recognition depends on the relevant framework and verified facts.
Source links:
- UN IPSAS Finance Manual, Chapter 8 — Assets donated to a project
- UN IPSAS Finance Manual, Chapter 3 — Contributions in kind
50. Q: How should the organization determine whether a receipt-control process is ready for independent review and ongoing monitoring?
A: Show answer
Readiness should be established through evidence that the full receipt population can be obtained, settlement and donor-matching controls operate, unresolved exceptions are assigned, allocation and acknowledgement approvals are controlled, and the accounting handoff reaches actual ledger posting evidence. The organization should test calculations, permissions, duplicate prevention, failed-interface recovery, reconciliation in both directions and the ability to retrieve the original source records. Reviewers should distinguish tests performed on fictional examples from tests performed against actual authorized systems and records. Remaining gaps should be recorded with their risks, accountable owners, deadlines and release implications. The process should enter operational use only after competent organizational authorities approve the framework, procedures, parameters, responsibilities and required evidence standards.
Study instruction:
- Assemble a readiness package covering source completeness, test results, unresolved issues, delegated approvals, system evidence and formal adoption status.
Key point:
- Readiness must be supported by test evidence and authorized decisions.
- Fictional cases and zero-variance examples do not prove real-world control effectiveness.
Source links:
- UN IPSAS Finance Manual, Chapter 18 — General Ledger controls and workflows
Rows: 50
Table 3
Grant Income Recognition and Accounting Controls
1. Q: What is the purpose and scope of a grant income recognition standard operating procedure (SOP)?
A: Show answer
A grant income recognition SOP establishes a controlled process for deciding whether, when, and at what amount grant income should be recognized. It connects the executed funding agreement, applicable accounting framework, recognition criteria, measurement evidence, independent review, approval, journal preparation, posting confirmation, reconciliation, and financial reporting. The SOP must distinguish accounting recognition from agreement approval, receipt processing, expenditure eligibility, partner advances, donor reporting, and grant closure. Those activities may belong to separate procedures, even though they provide evidence for the recognition assessment. The supplied SOP 013 example is explicitly fictional and not adopted. It can support procedure design, but its assumed entity, framework, accounting entries, controls, and examples must not be treated as approved organizational policy.

Study instruction:
- "Map the complete recognition process from agreement intake through approved accounting entry, reconciliation, and reporting, identifying which adjacent procedures own the other activities."
Key point:
- Recognition decisions require documented accounting analysis, evidence, and approval.
- A demonstration SOP is not an authorized policy or posting instruction.
Source links:
- UN IPSAS Corporate Guidance — Funding Arrangements
2. Q: Why must the applicable legal entity, reporting purpose, and accounting framework be established before grant income is recognized?
A: Show answer
Accounting conclusions depend on the reporting entity, reporting obligations, adopted framework, and applicable version of the requirements. An international NGO operating in Indonesia must not assume that IPSAS, full IFRS, Indonesian Financial Accounting Standards, or a donor's reporting basis automatically governs its financial statements. Determine the legal entity, reporting perimeter, statutory obligations, audit requirements, governing documents, approved accounting policies, and relevant agreement terms. An authorized decision-maker must approve the applicable framework and reporting period. If material requirements conflict or the framework remains unverified, mark the assessment as HOLD and prevent final recognition, account mapping, and posting until the issue is resolved. Donor reporting requirements may coexist with, but do not automatically replace, financial statement accounting requirements.

Study instruction:
- "Prepare a framework applicability register showing the legal entity, reporting basis, authoritative requirements, approval evidence, and unresolved decisions."
Key point:
- Accounting recognition rules must follow the framework applicable to the reporting entity.
- An unresolved framework is a substantive control issue, not an administrative formality.
Source links:
- IPSASB — Revenue and IPSAS 47
3. Q: How should the organization determine which accounting authority governs a particular funding arrangement?
A: Show answer
First establish the applicable financial reporting framework and reporting period. If IPSAS applies and has been adopted, assess the arrangement under the current applicable IPSAS requirements, including IPSAS 47 for revenue where relevant. The UN IPSAS Finance Manual's Chapter 3 and its Corporate Guidance on Funding Arrangements provide valuable explanations of non-exchange funding, conditions, restrictions, recognition, and operational processes; however, the legacy guidance based on IPSAS 23 should not automatically replace the applicable current standard. If IFRS applies, assess whether IAS 20, IFRS 15, or another standard governs the arrangement. The governing source register should record the source title, exact provision, version or edition, effective period, applicability conclusion, reviewer, and any superseded guidance. A rule should not be marked verified merely because a document exists.

Study instruction:
- "Trace one funding arrangement to the exact standard and applicable provision, then document why alternative standards do or do not govern it."
Key point:
- Authority selection precedes recognition analysis.
- Historical manuals provide context but must be reconciled with current applicable requirements.
Source links:
- UN IPSAS Finance Manual, Chapter 3 — Revenue from Non-Exchange Transactions
- IPSASB — IPSAS 47, Revenue
4. Q: How should grants, donations, customer contracts, pledges, and other funding arrangements be classified before recognition?
A: Show answer
Classification begins with the substance of the arrangement rather than its title or the identity of the funder. Examine the parties, binding rights and obligations, consideration, purpose, attached conditions, payment terms, deliverables, eligible-cost provisions, refund rights, and termination clauses. A private foundation grant, a government grant, a service contract, an informal pledge, a partner advance, and an inter-entity transfer can require different accounting analyses. Record the classification rationale and the authority that supports it. Where facts remain unclear, maintain an intake record and refer the matter for technical accounting or legal review. The organization should not force every receipt into a generic grant-income category simply because it originates from a funding organization.

Study instruction:
- "Classify a government grant, private donation, service contract, nonbinding pledge, and refundable advance using actual contractual facts and the applicable framework."
Key point:
- The substance and enforceable terms of the arrangement drive classification.
- Funding source labels alone are insufficient evidence of revenue recognition.
Source links:
- UN IPSAS Corporate Guidance — Funding Arrangements
5. Q: What evidence is needed to establish that a grant agreement creates an enforceable entitlement?
A: Show answer
Collect the executed agreement, relevant schedules, authorized signatures, approval evidence, applicable governing terms, and all amendments affecting the rights or obligations. Determine whether the parties are legally committed, whether further approvals remain outstanding, whether acceptance or commencement conditions apply, and whether any provisions make the funding contingent. Where enforceability is uncertain, obtain appropriate legal advice and document the conclusion. A signed document is important evidence, but the signature alone does not establish that every accounting recognition criterion has been met. Similarly, a budget allocation or award notification may indicate funding intent without establishing a present enforceable right. Link the assessment to a specific executed agreement version and retain the exact clause references used in the decision.

Study instruction:
- "Inspect a signed agreement and identify the clauses, approvals, and legal facts that establish or prevent a present enforceable entitlement."
Key point:
- Agreement execution and accounting recognition are separate decisions.
- Entitlement must be supported by the actual enforceable terms and applicable accounting requirements.
Source links:
- UN IPSAS Corporate Guidance — Funding Arrangements
6. Q: How should a nonbinding pledge, fundraising announcement, or conditional award notice be treated?
A: Show answer
Record the communication in the funding pipeline and agreement intake register, but do not automatically recognize income or a receivable. Determine whether the communication creates a binding commitment under the applicable law and accounting framework, whether acceptance is required, and whether future committee approval or another substantive decision remains outstanding. Obtain the executed agreement or other evidence needed to support the conclusion. If the evidence is incomplete, place the recognition assessment on HOLD and assign a responsible person and follow-up date. The absence of current recognition does not mean the communication should disappear from operational monitoring: anticipated funding may still be relevant to cash planning, forecasts, and management reporting if clearly identified as unconfirmed.

Study instruction:
- "Compare a nonbinding fundraising notice with an executed funding agreement and identify the additional evidence needed before recognition can be assessed."
Key point:
- An expected donation is not automatically an accounting asset or income.
- Pipeline tracking must remain separate from recognized balances.
Source links:
- UN IPSAS Finance Manual, Chapter 3 — Revenue from Non-Exchange Transactions
7. Q: Why is receiving cash not, by itself, sufficient to establish grant income?
A: Show answer
Cash receipt confirms that funds entered the bank or cash account; it does not resolve whether the amount meets the applicable recognition criteria. The organization must separately assess the arrangement, rights, conditions, restrictions, refund requirements, and relevant reporting period. Depending on the facts and framework, the corresponding accounting may involve recognized income, a liability or refundable balance, settlement of an existing receivable, or another appropriate classification. Receipt processing and income recognition should therefore have separate control steps and evidence. The bank statement, remittance advice, and receipt reference establish the cash movement, while the approved recognition assessment establishes the accounting conclusion. A receipt must not automatically trigger a revenue journal merely because a system can generate one.

Study instruction:
- "Trace a cash receipt through the bank statement, funding agreement, receipt record, recognition assessment, and general ledger to determine whether the income classification is supported."
Key point:
- Cash movement, entitlement, and income recognition are different accounting events.
- Every receipt must be classified using the applicable framework and the underlying agreement.
Source links:
- UN IPSAS Finance Manual, Chapter 3 — Revenue from Non-Exchange Transactions
8. Q: What is the difference between a recognition condition, a donor-purpose restriction, a milestone, and a payment schedule?
A: Show answer
A recognition condition may affect whether resources can be retained or whether an obligation must be satisfied before income is recognized, depending on the applicable framework. A purpose restriction describes how funding may be used or monitored, but does not automatically create a liability. A milestone may provide evidence of performance or another contractual trigger, although its accounting meaning must be established from the complete arrangement. A payment schedule specifies when cash is due, not necessarily when income is recognized. Reporting covenants, eligible-cost tests, termination clauses, and refund provisions also require individual analysis. The SOP should classify each material clause, record the supporting facts, determine its accounting consequence, and refer unresolved interpretations to an independent technical reviewer rather than treating all clauses as equivalent.

Study instruction:
- "Prepare a clause analysis showing how four hypothetical provisions—purpose restriction, substantive condition, milestone, and payment date—affect recognition, measurement, payment, and reporting separately."
Key point:
- Conditions, restrictions, milestones, and payment terms are not interchangeable.
- A donor clause must be interpreted under the applicable accounting requirements and actual facts.
Source links:
- UN IPSAS Corporate Guidance — Funding Arrangements
9. Q: When is IAS 20 relevant to grant income recognition?
A: Show answer
IAS 20 is relevant only when the reporting framework is IFRS Accounting Standards and the arrangement falls within the standard's scope as a government grant or government assistance. Under IAS 20, a government grant is recognized only when there is reasonable assurance that the entity will comply with its attached conditions and that the grant will be received. An income-related grant is recognized systematically over the periods in which the entity recognizes the related costs intended to be compensated, subject to the detailed requirements and facts. Consequently, signature, cash receipt, or a donor-purpose restriction alone does not settle the accounting. Private foundation donations and other non-government funding should not automatically be forced into IAS 20. The SOP must document scope, recognition evidence, the related costs, presentation policy, and review conclusions.

Study instruction:
- "Apply the IAS 20 scope test to a government grant and identify the evidence required for reasonable assurance and systematic recognition over related costs."
Key point:
- IAS 20 is not a universal accounting model for all NGO grants.
- Grant income must follow the actual standard's requirements and the arrangement's circumstances.
Source links:
- IFRS Foundation — IAS 20, Accounting for Government Grants and Disclosure of Government Assistance
10. Q: How should an NGO assess grant income under IPSAS when IPSAS is its applicable reporting framework?
A: Show answer
First confirm that IPSAS is the framework adopted by the reporting entity and determine the applicable edition and reporting period. For an entity using IPSAS, IPSAS 47 Revenue is the current revenue standard with an effective date for periods beginning on or after 1 January 2026, with earlier application permitted. The standard replaced the previous IPSAS revenue standards, including IPSAS 23. Analyze the particular funding arrangement under the applicable IPSAS 47 requirements rather than assuming that every grant follows the same recognition model. The UN Finance Manual remains useful for understanding UN funding arrangements and the operating evidence needed in practice, but its older IPSAS 23-based explanations must be read in light of the currently applicable standard. Record the relevant model, trigger, amount, obligation, and supporting evidence in the decision file.

Study instruction:
- "Compare the applicable current IPSAS revenue requirements with the legacy UN Finance Manual guidance and document the standard that governs the actual reporting period."
Key point:
- IPSAS 47 replaced IPSAS 23 for applicable periods beginning on or after 1 January 2026.
- The reporting entity's framework and adoption position must be verified before applying IPSAS guidance.
Source links:
- IPSASB — Revenue and IPSAS 47 effective date
- UN IPSAS Finance Manual, Chapter 3 — Legacy non-exchange revenue guidance
11. Q: When should IFRS 15 be considered for a funding agreement containing deliverables or milestones?
A: Show answer
IFRS 15 applies to revenue from contracts with customers within its scope; it should not be selected merely because a donor agreement mentions deliverables, reports, outputs, or milestones. Establish whether the counterparty is a customer obtaining goods or services that are outputs of the entity's ordinary activities in exchange for consideration. If IFRS applies and the arrangement qualifies, analyze the contract, performance obligations, transaction price, allocation, and timing of satisfaction of those obligations in accordance with the standard. If the arrangement is a non-exchange grant, another accounting model may be appropriate. Where the classification remains unresolved, record the alternatives, material facts, and required technical review, and do not calculate revenue solely by counting completed milestones.

Study instruction:
- "Analyze a donor arrangement with three milestones and determine whether IFRS 15 scope is established before calculating any revenue amount."
Key point:
- Milestones do not automatically establish that a donor is a customer.
- Scope must be resolved before applying a revenue recognition model.
Source links:
- IFRS Foundation — IFRS 15, Revenue from Contracts with Customers
12. Q: How should an unconditional right to grant funding be distinguished from a receivable and from cash already received?
A: Show answer
Determine first whether the arrangement creates a present enforceable right, whether the applicable recognition criteria have been met, and whether the amount can be measured appropriately. If the criteria for a receivable are met, recognize and measure the receivable under the applicable framework even when cash has not arrived, subject to relevant provisions and other requirements. When the cash is later collected, account for settlement of the receivable rather than recognizing the same funding a second time. If the agreement establishes only a future expectation without a qualifying present right, do not create a receivable merely from the award ceiling. A grant schedule should distinguish recognized income, receivables, cash receipts, advances, potentially refundable amounts, and unrecognized commitments.

Study instruction:
- "Reconcile an executed agreement, a qualifying receivable, the later bank receipt, and the corresponding ledger entries without double-counting income."
Key point:
- Receivables represent qualifying rights to receive amounts, while cash represents funds actually received.
- Recognition and collection must be separately tracked and reconciled.
Source links:
- UN IPSAS Finance Manual, Chapter 13 — Accounts Receivable
13. Q: How should a grant covering multiple years be recognized across reporting periods?
A: Show answer
Create a period-by-period schedule linked to the agreement, recognition basis, relevant conditions, estimated or actual related costs where applicable, and any substantive changes. Do not recognize the entire award merely because the grant is signed, approved internally, invoiced, or paid. Under IAS 20, when that standard applies, income-related grants are recognized systematically over periods in which the related costs are recognized. Other frameworks and arrangements require their own analysis. Cash installments, annual budgets, and reporting dates are useful schedule attributes, but they do not necessarily determine income timing. Every period's calculation should be supported by the recognition model, current facts, measurement inputs, reviewer sign-off, and any prior-period reconciliation.

Study instruction:
- "Build a multi-year grant schedule that separately tracks total award value, payments due, cash received, income recognized by period, and remaining balances."
Key point:
- A multi-year award needs a supported recognition schedule.
- Cash installments and accounting recognition periods are not automatically identical.
Source links:
- IFRS Foundation — IAS 20, Government Grants
- UN IPSAS Corporate Guidance — Funding Arrangements
14. Q: How should milestone-based and eligible-cost funding be assessed before recognizing income?
A: Show answer
Identify whether the agreement uses milestones, eligible expenditure, outputs, services, other substantive conditions, or only payment events. For each relevant clause, record the criterion, evidence required to demonstrate satisfaction, measurement basis, period, and approval responsibility. Evidence may include validated expenditure transactions, accepted deliverables, certificates, or other corroboration expressly required by the agreement and applicable model. A completed milestone count is not sufficient if the criteria for acceptance or the accounting model remain unresolved. If two of three milestones have been accepted for IDR 100,000,000 each, the arithmetic amount of IDR 200,000,000 is only a calculation candidate; it is not automatically recognized revenue until applicable scope and recognition criteria are verified.

Study instruction:
- "Trace eligible costs or milestone claims to the underlying records and verify that calculation, evidence, acceptance, and accounting treatment all follow the actual agreement."
Key point:
- Eligible-cost and milestone evidence must meet defined contractual and accounting criteria.
- A correct arithmetic total does not prove that revenue is recognizable.
Source links:
- IFRS Foundation — IFRS 15, Revenue from Contracts with Customers
15. Q: How should donor-purpose restrictions be accounted for and reported separately from income recognition?
A: Show answer
Record the restriction's purpose, duration, eligible activities, geographic or project limits, reporting requirements, and monitoring responsibility in a dedicated register. Then determine whether the applicable accounting framework treats any provision as a recognition condition, another obligation, a presentation requirement, or a restriction that should be monitored separately. Do not automatically record a liability or deferred income merely because a donor specifies that funds support education, health, or another approved purpose. Equally, do not ignore substantive conditions or refund requirements. The organization's restricted-fund schedule and accounting ledger may have different dimensions, so reconcile them and explain the relationship in management and donor reporting. The actual donor agreement and applicable accounting policy govern the conclusion.

Study instruction:
- "Compare a purpose-restricted grant with an agreement containing an unsatisfied substantive condition and explain the different recognition and monitoring implications."
Key point:
- Donor restrictions and accounting recognition conditions require separate analysis.
- Restricted-fund tracking does not automatically determine the financial statement liability balance.
Source links:
- UN IPSAS Corporate Guidance — Funding Arrangements
16. Q: How should the amount of grant income be measured after the recognition criteria are satisfied?
A: Show answer
Determine the recognized amount using the applicable accounting model, contractual terms, valid measurement inputs, and period-specific evidence. Maintain the calculation formula, currency, unit of account, estimated or actual inputs, supporting references, rounding policy, preparer, and independent recalculation. Award ceilings, installment values, budgets, and expenditure totals should not be substituted for the required measurement basis without justification. Variable amounts, eligibility adjustments, exchange differences, refund provisions, and other material factors must be considered when the applicable requirements demand them. Unapproved assumptions or unresolved contractual terms should keep the measurement on HOLD. Once the basis is validated, retain the calculations and evidence as part of the approval-bound assessment version.

Study instruction:
- "Recalculate a grant amount independently from the approved measurement basis and reconcile it to the agreement, schedule, and supporting transactions."
Key point:
- Recognition amount must be derived from the applicable accounting model, not from an untested spreadsheet assumption.
- Measurement inputs and estimates must be traceable and independently reviewable.
Source links:
- UN IPSAS Finance Manual, Chapter 3 — Non-Exchange Revenue
17. Q: How should foreign-currency grants be measured and converted into the organization's functional currency?
A: Show answer
Establish the applicable framework's foreign-currency requirements and the organization's approved exchange-rate policy before calculating the accounting amount. Retain the original currency, original amount, rate, rate direction, effective date, source, conversion formula, rounding rule, and evidence of approval. Distinguish transaction-date measurement, subsequent remeasurement of monetary balances, settlement differences, and any relevant donor-reporting translation rules. Do not use a convenient spreadsheet rate or an illustrative rate from an example as evidence of an approved live rate. Independently recalculate the result and investigate differences before approval. If the required source or date cannot be verified, hold the affected calculation rather than inserting an unexplained balancing amount.

Study instruction:
- "Recalculate a foreign-currency grant measurement from the verified rate source and independently test the currency direction, date, and rounding."
Key point:
- Currency translation requires an authorized policy and supported rate evidence.
- An arithmetically correct conversion does not by itself validate the accounting conclusion.
Source links:
- IPSASB — Revenue and applicable measurement requirements
18. Q: How should grant-related taxes, indirect costs, and project allocations be treated in the recognition process?
A: Show answer
First separate the income recognition assessment from tax compliance, expenditure eligibility, and cost allocation. Determine whether the arrangement's stated amount is gross or net of relevant taxes, whether the entity has a separate tax obligation or recoverable tax balance, and whether indirect costs are included in the grant ceiling or calculated under an approved recovery mechanism. Use the applicable tax law, accounting framework, grant terms, and organizational policy to decide each treatment. Project and donor allocations must be supported by an approved methodology and reconciled to the source amount so the same costs or income are not duplicated. Tax-sensitive, cross-border, or ambiguous arrangements should be referred to the appropriate specialist before final approval.

Study instruction:
- "Trace a grant calculation involving gross funding, a tax-related balance, indirect costs, and two project codes, documenting the distinct accounting and donor-compliance decisions."
Key point:
- Recognition, tax accounting, and donor cost allocation are related but distinct assessments.
- Tax and indirect-cost treatment must follow verified requirements and actual agreement terms.
Source links:
- UN IPSAS Corporate Guidance — Funding Arrangements
19. Q: How should the grant recognition schedule distinguish total award value, income recognized, installments, and remaining balances?
A: Show answer
Maintain separate fields for total approved award value, enforceable entitlement, amounts invoiced or due, cash received, income recognized to date, future scheduled amounts, refundable or advance balances, and unresolved items. Each amount should be supported by the relevant agreement version and its accounting basis. A schedule must not automatically release income according to the cash collection pattern or simply divide an award equally across the contract period. For each reporting period, show the applicable recognition trigger, calculation, cumulative recognized amount, remaining amount, and evidence status. Reconcile the schedule to receipts, receivables, liabilities, the general ledger, and donor reporting where relevant. Schedule-generated journals should remain proposals until all recognition and approval gates are satisfied.

Study instruction:
- "Build a roll-forward for a grant showing opening balances, changes, income recognized, cash collected, outstanding receivables, and remaining scheduled amounts."
Key point:
- Award value, cash, receivables, and recognized income are separate schedule dimensions.
- A schedule supports accounting but must not approve or post entries automatically.
Source links:
- UN IPSAS Finance Manual, Chapter 13 — Accounts Receivable
20. Q: What should a grant agreement intake and clause analysis checklist contain?
A: Show answer
The intake form should capture a unique arrangement ID, legal entity, funder, project, agreement title and version, execution and effective dates, total amount, currencies, authorized signatories, reporting period, and relevant account dimensions. The clause analysis should index substantive conditions, eligible-cost terms, purpose restrictions, performance obligations, milestones, payment dates, reporting covenants, amendment provisions, refund rights, and termination clauses. Each clause should have a summary, exact document reference, accounting question, evidence required, reviewer, conclusion, and outstanding action. Missing documents, unresolved authority, contradictory terms, and unclear enforceability should trigger a controlled HOLD status. The completed intake should be linked to the recognition assessment, approval, journal, reconciliation, and any referral to another process owner.

Study instruction:
- "Use one executed funding agreement to complete the intake form and create a clause register that connects each material provision to its assessment and evidence."
Key point:
- A standardized intake prevents material clauses from being overlooked.
- Each recognition conclusion must be traceable to a specific agreement version and provision.
Source links:
- UN IPSAS Corporate Guidance — Funding Arrangements
21. Q: Who should prepare, review, approve, and post a grant income recognition assessment?
A: Show answer
Assign separate responsibilities for requesting the assessment, maintaining the agreement master, preparing the accounting analysis, independently reviewing technical conclusions, approving within delegated authority, posting an authorized journal, reconciling balances, maintaining records, and monitoring controls. Program and grants personnel provide operational facts, while Finance determines accounting treatment under the applicable framework. Legal, tax, donor-compliance, or other specialists should be consulted when triggered by unresolved issues. The procedure must identify deputies, authority limits, review deadlines, and escalation paths. A role matrix is insufficient unless actual system permissions and approval records reflect it. In smaller teams, where complete segregation is impracticable, documented compensating controls such as independent review of calculations and posting evidence should be applied and tested.

Study instruction:
- "Prepare a responsibility matrix for one grant and verify that the agreement owner, preparer, independent reviewer, approver, posting operator, and reconciler have suitable responsibilities and permissions."
Key point:
- The preparer should not be the sole independent reviewer and approver.
- Actual permissions and delegated authority must match the approved responsibility matrix.
Source links:
- UN IPSAS Finance Manual, Chapter 18 — General Ledger Controls
22. Q: What controls should prevent self-approval and approval beyond a person's delegated authority?
A: Show answer
The approval register should record the approver, authority source, value and scope limits, effective dates, relevant entity, and any conditions or exclusions. Compare the approval request with those limits before release. The reviewer must be independent of the preparation where required, and the agreement negotiator or program owner should not alone determine accounting treatment. Approval must identify the exact assessment version, agreement version, recognized amount, currency, period, conclusion, and account coding reviewed. If a material fact changes after approval, the previous approval must be assessed for validity and renewed when necessary. Evidence of approval should include the authorized record or workflow event, timestamp, reviewer identity, and resolution of material comments rather than a verbal confirmation alone.

Study instruction:
- "Test a sample approval against authority limits and simulate a material amendment after approval to determine whether release is correctly blocked."
Key point:
- Approval must be issued by an authorized person for the exact transaction and version reviewed.
- Material changes can invalidate previously granted approval.
Source links:
- UN IPSAS Finance Manual, Chapter 18 — General Ledger Controls
23. Q: What documentation should an independent reviewer examine before approving grant income recognition?
A: Show answer
Review the applicable framework decision, agreement and amendments, clause register, evidence of binding entitlement, satisfaction of relevant conditions, measurement calculations, estimates, foreign-exchange inputs, coding, period, draft journal, and reconciliation. Investigate unexplained movements, unusual terms, manual adjustments, incomplete documents, and differences from prior periods. Where the arrangement contains significant uncertainty, require a documented technical conclusion or specialist clearance as appropriate. Confirm that the proposed entry follows the accounting assessment and that donor reporting, restrictions, receivables, cash, advances, and potential refundable balances are not conflated. Approval should record the reviewer's identity, date, assessment version, outstanding matters, and final conclusion. A signed checklist is not an adequate substitute for resolving material issues.

Study instruction:
- "Apply a reviewer checklist to an assessment and evidence how each material issue was resolved, corrected, referred, or retained on HOLD."
Key point:
- Review covers framework, recognition, measurement, coding, period, and evidence.
- Material questions require a documented resolution, not an unsupported verbal assurance.
Source links:
- UN IPSAS Corporate Guidance — Funding Arrangements
24. Q: How should grant income journal entries be prepared and mapped to the chart of accounts?
A: Show answer
Prepare the journal only after the recognition conclusion and amount are supported under the applicable accounting policy. Determine the appropriate income, asset, liability, receivable, or other accounts from the organization's approved chart of accounts and required dimensions. Include entity, donor, project, fund, currency, period, agreement ID, assessment version, and source-document references as appropriate. The journal should show the basis of calculation and clearly distinguish a new recognition event from collection of a receivable, release of a previously recognized liability, or correction of an earlier entry. Proposed debit and credit accounts must be independently reviewed before posting. No generic journal template should imply that all grant receipts debit cash and credit grant income, because the proper entry depends on the facts and prior transactions.

Study instruction:
- "Prepare draft entries for three distinct events—a qualifying recognition event, collection of an existing receivable, and receipt held pending satisfaction of conditions—and explain why the entries differ."
Key point:
- Journal entries depend on the accounting conclusion and transaction history.
- Approved chart-of-account mappings must be used rather than invented or defaulted codes.
Source links:
- UN IPSAS Finance Manual, Chapter 18 — General Ledger and Journal Vouchers
25. Q: What evidence is required before a grant income assessment can be marked as posted?
A: Show answer
Distinguish the stages of assessment prepared, under review, approved, submitted for posting, posted, and reconciled. A generated journal file, uploaded spreadsheet, submission response, or draft posting request does not necessarily prove that the journal reached the authoritative finance ledger. A POSTED status should require the finance-system journal reference, confirmed posting date, actual debit and credit lines, period, currency, dimensions, and evidence that the entry agrees with the approved assessment. If a posting fails or returns an uncertain or partial result, inspect the actual ledger state before retrying. This prevents duplicate entries and preserves the relationship between the original request, any failed attempt, the successful entry, and reconciliation results.

Study instruction:
- "Trace one approved assessment through the posting system and verify the actual journal lines before marking its status as POSTED."
Key point:
- A prepared or submitted journal is not proof of a successful ledger posting.
- Partial or uncertain posting states require investigation before any retry.
Source links:
- UN IPSAS Finance Manual, Chapter 18 — General Ledger Processes
26. Q: How should a signed amendment, extension, reduction, or cancellation affect the recognition assessment?
A: Show answer
Link every material amendment to the original agreement and assessment rather than replacing the historical record. Verify the authority of the amendment, its execution date, effective date, revised amount, altered terms, and consequences for current and prior periods. Determine whether the change affects recognition criteria, future amounts, estimates, account balances, donor reporting, disclosures, or an existing receivable. A revised award ceiling does not automatically establish the amount of an adjustment to recognized income. Material changes should create a new linked assessment and trigger review and reapproval of the affected conclusion and journal. Maintain both the original agreement and the amendment so an auditor can reconstruct why the recorded amount changed.

Study instruction:
- "Compare an executed amendment with the original agreement, calculate the change in award value, and document why the income effect requires a separate accounting assessment."
Key point:
