- Amendments require reassessment based on their actual terms and effective dates.
- Historical agreement versions and approvals must remain traceable.
Source links:
- UN IPSAS Corporate Guidance — Funding Arrangements
27. Q: How should potential refunds, clawbacks, and allegations of donor noncompliance be assessed?
A: Show answer
Obtain the relevant agreement clauses, factual evidence, donor correspondence, eligible-cost schedules, and details of any investigation or disputed expenditure. Determine whether a present obligation, refund, reduction of income, provision, contingent liability, or other accounting outcome is required under the applicable framework and facts. An allegation alone does not automatically establish a liability or justify reversing income. Equally, a material refund exposure must not be ignored merely because the donor has not issued a final demand. Refer legal, donor-compliance, and technical accounting questions to the appropriate specialists, document the decision and estimated impact where relevant, and preserve the original recognition and posting records. Reconcile any approved correction or payment with the original grant and subsequent reporting.

Study instruction:
- "Analyze a suspected ineligible grant expenditure and distinguish the allegation, verified facts, contractual refund trigger, accounting conclusion, and corrective action."
Key point:
- Refund and clawback accounting requires evidence and assessment of actual obligations.
- Corrections must preserve the original decision and a traceable history.
Source links:
- UN IPSAS Finance Manual, Chapter 15 — Provisions, Contingent Liabilities and Contingent Assets
28. Q: How should an accounting estimate change be distinguished from a prior-period error or an agreement modification?
A: Show answer
Examine why the recorded amount must change. An estimate may change because new reliable information becomes available; an error may result from failing to use or incorrectly using information that was available when earlier statements were prepared; an agreement modification changes the contractual facts; and a policy change follows a different governed decision process. Their accounting treatment, effective period, approval, and disclosure implications may differ under the applicable framework. Preserve the original assessment, calculation, evidence, and approval. Record the new facts, reason for the change, period affected, proposed adjustment, and reviewer decision. Do not automatically treat every variance as a current-period adjustment or rewrite historical records without assessing the requirements for correction and restatement.

Study instruction:
- "Compare four grant adjustments arising from new information, an earlier calculation error, a signed amendment, and a revised accounting policy, identifying the correct assessment route for each."
Key point:
- Different causes of change require different accounting analyses.
- Historical records must retain the original facts and the approved correction trail.
Source links:
- UN IPSAS Finance Manual, Chapter 17 — Accounting Policies, Changes in Estimates and Errors
29. Q: How should grant income recognition be controlled at month-end and year-end cut-off?
A: Show answer
Identify agreements, amendments, conditions, milestones, related costs, receipts, receivables, and corrections that may affect the reporting date. Verify the dates and events that determine recognition under the applicable model rather than simply using the invoice date, bank date, signature date, or reporting date. Reconcile the schedule and ledger, investigate late documentation and unusual movements, and identify assessments awaiting approval or evidence. Open material matters should be resolved or formally escalated before close, with a named owner and target date. The close pack should show the actual status as at the reporting date and separately identify evidence received later. Do not backdate a journal or override a closed period without the authorized procedure and a documented accounting basis.

Study instruction:
- "Test grant agreements and amendments close to period-end and document the event, evidence date, recognition basis, and approved period for each material item."
Key point:
- Cut-off depends on the recognition requirements and underlying facts.
- Late or unresolved material items require escalation, not silent deferral.
Source links:
- UN IPSAS Finance Manual, Chapter 17 — Policies, Estimates and Errors
30. Q: How should grant schedules be reconciled to agreements, bank receipts, receivables, the general ledger, and donor reports?
A: Show answer
Define the complete population of executed agreements, amendments, receipts, receivables, and assessments for the reporting period. Record extraction dates, source systems, transaction counts, duplicate checks, expected and actual totals, unmatched items, and reviewer evidence. Then reconcile the recognition schedule to the appropriate ledger accounts and related schedules, including advances, refundable balances, liabilities, or restrictions where applicable. Investigate each discrepancy, document its cause, and obtain approval for necessary corrections. A zero numerical variance does not demonstrate completeness if an agreement, bank account, or source population was omitted. Donor-report totals may follow specific contractual definitions, so differences from financial statement revenue must be explained and supported rather than forced to agree artificially.

Study instruction:
- "Perform a two-way reconciliation from the signed-agreement population to the general ledger and from reported grant income back to its underlying agreement and evidence."
Key point:
- Reconciliation must prove both numerical agreement and population completeness.
- Donor reports and financial statement balances may differ for legitimate, documented reasons.
Source links:
- UN IPSAS Finance Manual, Chapter 18 — General Ledger and Reconciliation Controls
31. Q: What accounting lesson can be learned from the SOP example of an IDR 1.2 billion government grant?
A: Show answer
The fictional casebook describes a government grant of IDR 1,200,000,000 intended to support costs of the same amount during 2026. It assumes that IDR 600,000,000 of related costs have been recognized through 30 June 2026 and calculates an illustrative amount of IDR 600,000,000 using a proportional calculation. That example is only an arithmetic demonstration under an explicitly hypothetical IFRS and IAS 20 assumption. The amount is not a universal formula or proof of an actual accounting conclusion. An entity must establish IAS 20 scope, assess reasonable assurance and the relevant conditions, confirm the grant's relationship to qualifying costs, apply the required recognition method, and verify the actual receipt and ledger history. The example's proposed journal was not posted to a real system and does not establish an approved chart-of-accounts treatment.

Study instruction:
- "Recalculate the fictional IDR 600,000,000 result and identify all framework, scope, condition, related-cost, measurement, review, and posting checks needed before applying a similar method in practice."
Key point:
- The arithmetic in an illustration is not itself an accounting policy.
- The example's amounts, approvals, and ledger references are fictional and not real transaction evidence.
Source links:
- IFRS Foundation — IAS 20, Government Grants
32. Q: What should happen when a signed award requires committee approval that has not yet been evidenced?
A: Show answer
The assessment should remain on HOLD until the relevant approval condition and its accounting significance have been verified. Obtain the agreement clause, required committee decision, evidence of approval if it exists, and any related correspondence. Do not recognize income from signature alone if the applicable model requires a substantive condition to be met and evidence is missing. If funds have already been received, the receipt must still be recorded through the responsible cash process and separately assessed for the appropriate balance-sheet treatment. Assign the missing evidence to an accountable person and a target date, include the arrangement in completeness monitoring, and prevent an unresolved assessment from passing approval or posting controls.

Study instruction:
- "Use the fictional case of an award awaiting committee approval to trace the HOLD status, missing evidence, follow-up owner, and separate handling of any cash receipt."
Key point:
- An unsatisfied substantive condition can block recognition under the applicable model.
- An unresolved assessment must remain visible in the control register until properly resolved.
Source links:
- UN IPSAS Corporate Guidance — Funding Arrangements
33. Q: How should donated goods and services be evaluated when they are supplied under a grant?
A: Show answer
Distinguish a promise to provide goods from actual receipt and control of those goods. Do not recognize a promised asset merely because it appears in the agreement. Once goods are delivered, assess whether an asset and related income or other accounting effect meet the applicable recognition criteria and determine the relevant measurement basis, supported by observable market evidence or independent valuation when required. Donated services require separate treatment under the entity's applicable framework and policies; recognition or disclosure must not be assumed to follow automatically from the treatment of goods. Coordinate the recognition assessment with inventory or fixed-asset procedures, custody records, asset control evidence, valuation, and financial reporting. Any thresholds or valuation rules from the UN or another organization must not be adopted as universal NGO policy.

Study instruction:
- "Compare promised tablets, delivered tablets, and donated professional services, documenting receipt evidence, control, valuation, applicable recognition requirements, and disclosure implications."
Key point:
- Promised goods, received assets, and donated services may require different accounting treatment.
- Recognition, valuation, custody, and disclosure must follow applicable requirements.
Source links:
- UN IPSAS Finance Manual, Chapter 3 — Goods and Services in Kind
- UN IPSAS Finance Manual, Chapter 8 — Property, Plant and Equipment
34. Q: How should an NGO distinguish grant income recognition from partner advances and grant expenditure accounting?
A: Show answer
Income received by the organization and transfers paid to implementing partners are different sides of funding arrangements. SOP 013's recognition assessment does not by itself determine partner expenditure, advance settlement, supplier eligibility, asset custody, or donor report preparation. Those activities require their own procedures and records. For a partner transfer, determine the applicable accounting treatment for the provider's payment and track advance balances and certified expenditure under the relevant policy. For grant income received, establish the entity's entitlement and recognition basis under its own applicable framework. Link the related schedules for reconciliation, but do not assume that an income entry settles a partner advance or that donor expenditure automatically equals recognized donor income.

Study instruction:
- "Map one received grant and one related transfer to an implementing partner, showing the separate recognition, payment, advance, expenditure, and reconciliation responsibilities."
Key point:
- Grant income recognition and partner transfer or advance accounting are distinct processes.
- Linked controls should reconcile related balances without collapsing their different accounting purposes.
Source links:
- UN IPSAS Finance Manual, Chapter 3 — Grants In and Grants Out
- IPSASB — IPSAS 48, Transfer Expenses
35. Q: How should inter-entity transfers and related-party funding movements be analyzed?
A: Show answer
Identify the legal entities on both sides of the transaction, the substance of the agreement, the direction of the transfer, whether the transaction is internal or external to the reporting entity, and the applicable consolidation and related-party requirements. Do not classify every movement between offices, affiliates, or consortium participants as grant income. Depending on the facts, it may represent an inter-entity receivable or payable, a contribution, an expense transfer, or another arrangement. Confirm whether the receiving entity has a qualifying right and whether the reporting entity controls the relevant resources. Maintain separate references to the original funding agreement, inter-entity terms, journal entries, and settlement. If the entity relationship or transaction substance is uncertain, escalate before recognition.

Study instruction:
- "Analyze a funding movement between two related organizations and document the legal parties, economic substance, applicable framework, and correct reporting route."
Key point:
- Transfers must be assessed from the actual entities' perspectives.
- Related-party or inter-entity cash movements are not automatically external grant income.
Source links:
- UN IPSAS Finance Manual, Chapter 3 — Funding Arrangements
36. Q: How should unresolved accounting, legal, tax, and donor-compliance questions be escalated?
A: Show answer
Maintain a controlled exception register that records the issue ID, arrangement, relevant clause, amount if known, affected period, source evidence, risk, owner, required specialist, hold scope, due date, and resolution evidence. Escalation should occur when the issue exceeds the preparer's authority, creates material measurement uncertainty, involves conflicting legal terms, or could affect compliance, financial statements, donor reporting, or a closed period. A referral should state the exact question and decision required rather than merely sending the entire file to another department. Do not clear the recognition or posting gate until the responsible specialist has documented the conclusion and any required approval has been obtained. Monitor overdue items and require evidence before closure.

Study instruction:
- "Create a referral for an unresolved refund clause and specify the accounting question, evidence index, owner, decision authority, deadline, and condition for re-entering the recognition workflow."
Key point:
- Unresolved material issues require a named decision-maker and a controlled hold.
- Escalation is complete only when the conclusion and supporting evidence are documented.
Source links:
- UN IPSAS Finance Manual, Chapter 15 — Provisions and Contingent Liabilities
37. Q: How should the organization retain grant recognition evidence and protect confidential donor information?
A: Show answer
Establish a retention schedule that considers applicable law, donor agreements, tax requirements, privacy obligations, contractual provisions, audit needs, and approved internal policy. Keep the executed agreement, amendments, assessment, calculations, approvals, journal references, reconciliation, exceptions, and reporting evidence linked through a consistent identifier. Restrict access according to role, especially for donor contracts, bank details, legal correspondence, and sensitive beneficiary or partner information. Do not store credentials, authentication tokens, or passwords in grant registers. Preserve the integrity and retrievability of approved records, use controlled versions, and ensure that backups and recovery arrangements are tested. Records must not be deleted merely because the grant is financially closed if retention duties or legal holds remain unresolved.

Study instruction:
- "Test whether an independent reviewer can retrieve a complete grant evidence pack and verify access permissions, retention status, version integrity, and backup recoverability."
Key point:
- Complete evidence and secure retention support accountability and auditability.
- Access and disposal must follow verified policy and applicable requirements.
Source links:
- UN IPSAS Finance Manual, Chapter 18 — General Ledger and Record Controls
38. Q: How can Excel, Power Query, and VBA support grant income recognition without replacing accounting judgment?
A: Show answer
Excel Tables can provide controlled registers for agreements, clauses, assessments, approvals, and journals. Power Query can combine source files, normalize agreement identifiers, detect duplicate records, standardize dates and currencies, and produce completeness and reconciliation reports. Formulas can apply approved calculations, while VBA can prepare draft journals, update workflow statuses, create an audit log, and export approved reports. However, an automated result does not prove that an arrangement meets recognition criteria or that the chosen accounting model is correct. Framework selection, clause interpretation, unresolved conditions, material estimates, and journal approval still require authorized review. Document query versions, calculation parameters, source extraction dates, test cases, and exceptions; independently recalculate material results before release.

Study instruction:
- "Design a controlled grant register and test it with missing agreements, duplicate records, unapproved exchange rates, unsatisfied conditions, and changes after approval."
Key point:
- Automation improves consistency and evidence capture but does not determine accounting validity.
- Technical controls must preserve the authority, review, and approval requirements of the recognition procedure.
Source links:
- IPSASB — Revenue and IPSAS 47
39. Q: How should automated tests prevent incorrect grant recognition and posting?
A: Show answer
Build positive and negative tests into the release process. Positive tests should confirm that a supported assessment passes each applicable gate and produces reproducible measurement results. Negative tests should reject missing agreement evidence, an unresolved framework, an unmet substantive condition, a duplicate assessment, unsupported foreign-exchange data, an unapproved account code, an expired delegation, stale approval after a material change, and posting without confirmed ledger evidence. Reconciliation tests must verify source completeness as well as numeric agreement. Exception closure should require correction, independent retesting, and authorized approval. Test results should identify the test ID, inputs, expected result, actual result, software version, reviewer, and release decision. An error message or workbook status must not be treated as proof that a control works unless the control has been tested.

Study instruction:
- "Create a test matrix showing the expected HOLD or rejection result for at least five common failure modes and retain evidence of successful retesting."
Key point:
- Negative tests are essential for preventing unsupported recognition and duplicate postings.
- Release requires evidence that controls block invalid transactions, not merely that the workbook runs.
Source links:
- UN IPSAS Finance Manual, Chapter 18 — General Ledger Controls
40. Q: What information should management receive to monitor grant income recognition and unresolved risks?
A: Show answer
A management report should summarize recognized income by period and funding source, amounts awaiting review, unresolved conditions, unconfirmed commitments, receivables, cash collected, advances or refundable balances, material amendments, and unreconciled differences. Include deadlines, aging, named owners, outstanding decisions, and risks that may affect financial statements or donor compliance. Distinguish confirmed values from estimates, draft calculations, and unapproved arrangements. Useful indicators include the proportion of assessments reviewed on time, aging of open exceptions, number of unsupported assessments, overdue reconciliations, post-approval changes, and duplicate-posting incidents. Define each metric's source, formula, reporting frequency, and escalation threshold. Management reporting should direct corrective action rather than merely display totals from a workbook.

Study instruction:
- "Create a grant recognition dashboard and reconcile its totals and status indicators to the approved assessment register, actual ledger postings, and exception log."
Key point:
- Management needs visibility over recognized amounts, outstanding decisions, and unresolved control risks.
- Dashboard values must be supported by authoritative records and clearly defined status rules.
Source links:
- UN IPSAS Finance Manual, Chapter 16 — Presentation and Disclosure
41. Q: How should financial statement presentation and disclosures be linked to grant income recognition?
A: Show answer
Ensure that income recognized in the ledger is presented under the applicable reporting framework and that related assets, liabilities, receivables, advances, and other balances are classified consistently. Identify disclosures required for material funding arrangements, accounting judgments, estimates, restrictions, and relevant uncertainties under the governing requirements. Donor reports often use project budgets or eligibility definitions that differ from financial statement classifications; these differences should be reconciled and explained. The reporting process must not assume that a management dashboard or donor report substitutes for the formal financial statements. Finance should maintain the link between the approved assessment, ledger account, reporting line, disclosure data, and review evidence so that a reported figure can be traced back to its supporting arrangement.

Study instruction:
- "Trace grant income and related balance-sheet accounts into the financial statement reporting pack and identify disclosures and reconciliations required by the applicable framework."
Key point:
- Recognition, classification, presentation, and disclosure must follow the applicable framework.
- Donor reporting and financial statement reporting serve different purposes and may require a documented reconciliation.
Source links:
- UN IPSAS Finance Manual, Chapter 16 — Presentation of Financial Statements and Disclosures
42. Q: What must be completed before a grant income recognition SOP can be adopted for operational use?
A: Show answer
Replace the fictional entity and assumptions with verified organizational facts, determine the applicable framework and reporting period, review current authoritative requirements, obtain the actual executed agreements and approved internal policies, and confirm account mappings and system responsibilities. Appoint the policy owner, independent reviewer, authorized approver, and operational process owners. Approve any internal deadlines, materiality criteria, foreign-exchange policies, rounding rules, retention requirements, and posting controls rather than leaving placeholders in an operational document. Test representative scenarios, exceptions, access permissions, audit trails, journal generation, and reconciliations. Record formal approval, version, effective date, training, and release evidence. Until those gates are completed, the supplied SOP example remains a fictional demonstration and must not authorize actual recognition decisions or postings.

Study instruction:
- "Prepare an adoption checklist that records each required organizational decision, responsible owner, authoritative evidence, testing result, approval, and effective date."
Key point:
- A draft becomes operational only after applicability, policy, control, testing, and approval requirements are satisfied.
- Fictional names, approvals, amounts, and system references must never be represented as actual organizational evidence.

Restricted Grants and Unspent Balance Controls
1. Q: What is the purpose of an SOP for restricted funds, grant advances, and unspent balances?
A: Show answer
The purpose is to establish a consistent, auditable process for interpreting donor restrictions, monitoring grant advances, assessing conditions, reconciling balances, determining whether repayment or refund obligations exist, and closing grants. The SOP should connect agreement evidence, operational delivery, accounting judgments, approvals, payment controls, donor reporting, and record retention. It must distinguish activities owned by Grants Finance from those owned by Legal, Accounting, Treasury, program teams, and payment processing. A completed reconciliation or balanced ledger does not establish contractual compliance or prove that a grant has been accounted for correctly. Each material decision must identify its evidence, responsible person, independent reviewer, authorization, and status. The example SOP supplied for this exercise is explicitly fictional and unapproved. Its organization, donor, amounts, agreement clauses, and evidence references must not be represented as actual organizational facts or approvals.

Study instruction:
- Review the SOP's objectives and identify the evidence required to trace a grant from agreement intake through final closeout.
Key point:
- The SOP establishes controls and decision gates, not automatic authorization to post or spend.
- Fictional examples must remain clearly distinguished from actual policies and records.

- UN IPSAS Finance Manual, Chapter 3 — Revenue from Non-Exchange Transactions
2. Q: Which activities belong within the restricted-funds SOP, and which require handoffs to other procedures?
A: Show answer
The SOP covers agreement-clause assessment, restrictions and conditions, grant balance monitoring, accounting decision coordination, unspent-balance analysis, refund assessment, period-end reconciliation, exceptions, and grant closeout. Agreement approval, initial award setup, receipt processing, procurement, expenditure coding, partner management, payment execution, and formal donor-report submission may be governed by separate procedures. The SOP should identify the responsible procedure, receiving owner, required input, expected output, deadline, and completion evidence for every handoff. For example, Grants Finance may determine that a refund assessment is required, but Treasury or the authorized payment owner must execute an approved payment through the payment procedure. Accounting determines the journal treatment under the applicable framework. Legal reviews contractual enforceability where needed. These boundaries prevent one team from silently assuming authority assigned to another.

Study instruction:
- Map each stage from award receipt through closeout to its governing SOP and responsible owner.
Key point:
- Process handoffs need named owners and documented evidence.
- A tracking procedure does not replace legal, accounting, payment, or reporting authorization.

3. Q: Why must the fictional SOP remain marked as a draft rather than an approved organizational policy?
A: Show answer
The supplied document uses a fictional organization, fictional donor, invented agreement clauses, invented amounts, and synthetic evidence identifiers. Its status is explicitly “FICTIONAL DEMONSTRATION — NOT ADOPTED,” and actual framework applicability, legal enforceability, accounting conclusions, and organizational approval remain unresolved. These limitations affect whether the document can be relied upon operationally. Before adoption, management must verify the organization's legal identity, reporting framework, current accounting policy, executed donor agreements, approval delegation, and applicable statutory requirements. The document owner must resolve placeholders, validate source references, test calculations and workflow gates, obtain independent review, and secure authorized approval. A sample approval identifier cannot substitute for an actual signed approval record. Until the adoption gate is passed, the file may support design and training but must not authorize expenditure, journals, refunds, or donor commitments.

4. Q: How should the organization determine which accounting framework governs grant recognition and unspent balances?
A: Show answer
Begin by establishing the reporting entity, applicable statutory requirements, accounting framework, reporting period, and approved accounting policies. Do not infer the framework merely because the organization is a foundation, uses particular software, receives funding from an international donor, or followed a certain method in a previous year. For an Indonesian foundation, the responsible finance officer should obtain the applicable authoritative Indonesian financial-reporting standards and confirm the appropriate standards tier with a qualified professional. The UN IPSAS Finance Manual is an important reference for UN grant processes, but it is not automatically binding on an independent Indonesian NGO. IPSAS 47 became effective for periods beginning on or after 1 January 2026 under the IPSASB's published implementation timetable. The current framework and any transition requirements must be verified before applying older guidance based on IPSAS 23. Keep framework applicability on HOLD until the required evidence and approval are recorded.

Study instruction:
- Prepare a framework assessment identifying the reporting entity, applicable standards, effective dates, authoritative sources, reviewer, and approval evidence.
Key point:
- Accounting treatment depends on the framework applicable to the reporting entity.
- A historical manual or example cannot establish framework applicability by itself.
Source links:
- IPSASB — Revenue and IPSAS 47
- IAI — Indonesian Financial Accounting Standards Effective from 1 January 2025
- UN IPSAS Finance Manual, Chapter 3 — Revenue from Non-Exchange Transactions
5. Q: What is the difference between a donor restriction and an accounting condition?
A: Show answer
A restriction generally describes the purpose, population, location, activity, or period for which funds may be used. A condition may affect entitlement to resources or create obligations under the applicable framework and agreement. The distinction must be assessed from the entire arrangement rather than from an isolated phrase. Review the donor's wording, performance requirements, evidence criteria, rights of return, enforceability, cure periods, and relationship between the requirement and the organization's entitlement. A restriction on using funds for teacher training, for example, does not automatically establish that the cash received is an accounting liability. A substantive milestone associated with a right to repayment or a future tranche may produce a different conclusion. Record the contractual assessment and accounting conclusion separately, and obtain qualified legal review when enforceability is ambiguous.

Study instruction:
- Compare a purpose-only restriction with a milestone and enforceable return clause; document the facts that distinguish them.
Key point:
- Restricted use and accounting liability are not interchangeable concepts.
- Conditions, entitlement, and enforceable rights must be evaluated under the applicable framework.
Source links:
- UN IPSAS Finance Manual, Chapter 3 — Donor Stipulations and Non-Exchange Revenue
- IPSASB — Revenue and IPSAS 47
6. Q: Why is the term “grant advance” insufficient to determine the accounting treatment?
A: Show answer
The term “advance” describes the commercial or operational arrangement but does not, by itself, establish whether the balance is an asset, liability, deferred amount, revenue, expense, or another category under the applicable framework. A grant received before an activity may involve a condition that remains unsatisfied, a restriction over use, a future performance requirement, or an obligation to return amounts under specified circumstances. The organization must assess the actual rights and obligations, the nature of the transaction, the applicable recognition criteria, and the reporting purpose. It must also distinguish grants received by the NGO from advances paid by the NGO to an implementing partner. An outgoing partner advance may remain an asset until eligible expenditure is substantiated or another applicable recognition event occurs. The accounting conclusion must be approved before posting, with the operational register preserving the related award and settlement information.

Study instruction:
- Examine one incoming grant and one outgoing partner advance, then identify the distinct accounting questions and evidence for each.
Key point:
- A transaction label is not a substitute for accounting analysis.
- Incoming grants and outgoing advances require different assessments.
Source links:
- UN IPSAS Finance Manual, Chapter 3 — Grants In and Grants Out
- UN IPSAS Finance Manual, Chapter 16 — Advance Receipts and Other Liabilities
7. Q: Why does receiving grant cash not automatically mean that the organization should recognize revenue immediately?
A: Show answer
A bank receipt proves that cash was received, subject to reconciliation and verification, but it does not independently establish the appropriate income-recognition treatment. The organization must determine whether the grant arrangement creates an enforceable entitlement, whether substantive conditions remain unfulfilled, and how the applicable framework treats the arrangement. A valid agreement may support recognition at a particular point, while another arrangement may require recognition to be deferred or handled differently. The receipt must be matched to the donor, award, tranche, currency, remittance reference, bank statement, and relevant agreement version. Cash, revenue, receivables, liabilities, restricted-purpose tracking, and budget figures should be recorded as separate concepts. Where the framework or contractual analysis remains unresolved, the accounting result must remain undetermined rather than being forced into revenue to make a report appear complete.

Study instruction:
- Trace a grant receipt from the bank statement to the agreement and explain why cash evidence and revenue recognition are separate decisions.
Key point:
- Receipt confirmation is not equivalent to revenue recognition.
- An approved accounting assessment must precede the journal handoff.
Source links:
- UN IPSAS Finance Manual, Chapter 3 — Revenue Recognition for Contributions
- UN IPSAS Finance Manual, Chapter 16 — Advance Receipts and Conditional Funding
8. Q: When can an expected grant tranche or donor reimbursement be recognized as a receivable?
A: Show answer
An expected payment is not automatically a receivable. Finance must assess whether the organization has a qualifying and sufficiently supported right to consideration under the applicable framework. Required evidence may include the executed agreement, payment schedule, submitted deliverables, eligible-cost claims, milestone completion, donor acceptance, and any applicable approval or certification. A budget forecast or informal expectation of further funding cannot replace evidence of the underlying right. Where a substantive entitlement barrier has not been satisfied, the receivable decision may need to remain on HOLD. The reviewer should document the amount, currency, expected timing, conditions, measurement basis, and unresolved uncertainties. Once the right is established, recognition and subsequent collection monitoring should follow the selected framework and approved policy. The status of a receivable must not be confused with the status of cash already received or income already recognized.

Study instruction:
- Review a proposed tranche receivable and test the executed agreement, entitlement evidence, measurement basis, and approval status.
Key point:
- A forecast or budget allocation does not alone establish a receivable.
- Entitlement and measurement require documentary support.
Source links:
- UN IPSAS Finance Manual, Chapter 13 — Contributions Receivable
- UN IPSAS Finance Manual, Chapter 3 — Contributions and Recognition
9. Q: How should an agreement clause requiring unused or unsupported funds to be returned be assessed?
A: Show answer
The review should identify the exact clause, agreement version, trigger event, calculation basis, deadline, cure period, dispute mechanism, waiver provision, and any amendments or correspondence affecting interpretation. Legal or Compliance should evaluate enforceability and the interaction of relevant clauses where the conclusion is material or ambiguous. Finance should separately determine the accounting consequences under the applicable framework and assess whether an amount can be measured reliably from verified facts. A possible right of return does not automatically establish the exact liability amount or payment date in every reporting context. Equally, the absence of a clearly worded refund clause should not be treated as proof that the organization can freely retain unused funds. Record unresolved conclusions as HOLD, preserve the contractual evidence, and prohibit unauthorized promises or payments until the relevant decision and authority requirements are satisfied.

Study instruction:
- Prepare a clause assessment showing the contractual trigger, legal review, measurement basis, accounting review, and required authorization.
Key point:
- Contractual enforceability and accounting recognition are related but distinct decisions.
- A refund must not be promised or processed from an unsupported calculation.
Source links:
- IFRS — IAS 37 Provisions, Contingent Liabilities and Contingent Assets
- UN IPSAS Finance Manual, Chapter 3 — Revenue and Return of Unused Funds
10. Q: Which agreement provisions should be reviewed when assessing a donor-funded grant?
A: Show answer
The assessment should cover permitted purposes and locations, award dates, budgets, eligible costs, conditions precedent and subsequent, milestones, deliverables, acceptance criteria, reporting obligations, rights of return, cure and dispute provisions, termination, asset ownership, interest, foreign-exchange treatment, reallocation, netting, amendments, and governing law. Every conclusion should reference the exact signed agreement version and applicable clause. Grant Finance records operational interpretations and coordinates donor clarification, while Legal reviews enforceability and Accounting evaluates recognition and measurement. An amendment should be accepted into the controlled record only after its effective status, authorized signatories, scope, and impact have been verified. Informal emails or unsigned clarifications must not silently replace executed terms. Preserve historical assessments so a reviewer can identify why an earlier decision was reasonable based on the evidence available at that time.

Study instruction:
- Build an agreement-clause inventory and ensure that every material clause has an owner, evidence requirement, assessment status, and review trigger.
Key point:
- Decisions must refer to the complete, effective agreement rather than an isolated sentence.
- Amendments and donor clarifications require controlled version management.

- UN IPSAS Finance Manual, Chapter 3 — Donor Agreement Accounting
11. Q: How should responsibility and segregation of duties be designed for grant accounting and refunds?
A: Show answer
The control design should distinguish the requesting program owner, Grants Finance, accounting policy owner, Legal or Compliance, independent reviewer, authorized approver, journal owner, payment owner, records administrator, and Internal Audit. A program owner may supply delivery evidence and forecasts but should not independently approve their own accounting conclusion or refund request. Finance may calculate a proposed disposition but should not be the sole authority for contractual enforceability. The person posting the journal should receive an approved accounting handoff rather than interpreting an unapproved draft. Refund entitlement, refund authorization, payment execution, and settlement reconciliation should remain distinct tasks. Where staffing limitations prevent full segregation, management should document a compensating independent review and test that it operates effectively. Role titles alone do not demonstrate current authority; scope, limits, effective dates, substitutes, and approval evidence must be recorded.

Study instruction:
- Prepare a responsibility matrix and test whether any individual can request, approve, post, pay, and reconcile the same material transaction without independent oversight.
Key point:
- Material decisions require independent challenge and documented authority.
- Payment execution must not determine refund entitlement.

12. Q: What documents should be collected when a new grant or amendment enters the process?
A: Show answer
The intake file should include the executed agreement, applicable amendments, donor identity and award reference, reporting entity, award period, purpose, currency, budget version, tranche schedule, condition register, authorized signatories, relevant correspondence, and existing accounting or donor policy references. Record a unique assessment ID, grant ID, agreement ID, version, owner, source references, and current status. Confirm which copies are original evidence and which are working copies. Missing material documents should be assigned an owner and deadline, with an explicit HOLD status for conclusions that depend on them. The intake process also needs to establish the appropriate record-access classification and retention rule. No assessment should use a placeholder internal finance manual as authority until the actual approved document, version, clause, and effective date are verified. Any subsequent change to source evidence should trigger an impact assessment and preserve the previous version.

Study instruction:
- Test a new-award intake file against a checklist and identify missing documents, unverified amendments, and fields that block the assessment.
Key point:
- Every conclusion must be traceable to identified source records and versions.
- Missing evidence requires an owner and documented follow-up, not an assumed answer.

- UN IPSAS Finance Manual, Chapter 3 — Donor Agreements and Contributions
13. Q: What fields should a grant balance register contain?
A: Show answer
A controlled register should identify the grant, donor, agreement version, reporting period, currency, approved budget, opening balances, receipts, cash disbursements, eligible actual costs, approved commitments, forecast final costs, conditions, receivables, recognized income, liabilities, restricted-purpose tracking, potential refunds, and closeout status. Each balance should have a defined calculation or authoritative source, responsible owner, reviewer, and reconciliation reference. Keep both source values and derived values where needed so calculations can be independently reproduced. The register should record source-file versions, extraction dates, approval status, and unresolved exceptions. It must distinguish operational tracking amounts from figures approved for accounting or external reporting. For pooled cash, grant-level cash attribution can be tracked without implying that a separately held bank account exists. The register is a control tool; the general ledger and official bank records remain separate records requiring reconciliation.

Study instruction:
- Design a register data dictionary with field definitions, owners, approved sources, formulas, and reconciliation requirements.
Key point:
- One grant register can coordinate information without collapsing different accounting and operational measures.
- Derived balances must be reproducible from traceable source data.
